# exp14 protocol: a cascade with a router trained on iteration benefit (pre-registered 2026-11-15)

## Why

exp13 established three things:
- **4-hop gain is real:** conditioning-trained iteration beats matched one-shot ranking on fresh 4-hop chains (+0.055, p = 0.035).
- **The 4-hop cascade failed:** its router predicts *hop count*, not *where iteration helps*.
- **3-hop gains too (exploratory):** on fresh dev questions, iteration also helps 3-hop chains.

**Question:** can a cheap router, trained directly on per-question benefit, capture iteration's gain at a fraction of its cost?

## Models (frozen, exp12 GPU seed 0)

- **One-shot:** M1-ctrl (`m1_ctrl`), 20 passes per question.
- **Iterative:** M1-iter (`m1_iter_s0`), about 74 passes.
- **The cascade:**
  - every question gets the one-shot pass
  - routed questions also get the iterative pass, about 20 + 74
  - routed questions use the iterative ranking
- exp13's rankings of `router_train` and `dev_fresh` were made with these same models (sha recorded) and are reused.

## Data (`split_ids.json`, fixed before any scoring; seed 14)

Drawn from the 13,538 MuSiQue-Ans train questions **never used** in exp08â€“exp13 (natural hop mix: about 74% 2-hop, 22% 3-hop, 3â€“4% 4-hop):

| Split | n | Use |
|---|---|---|
| `b_train` | 4,000 | label benefit (run both rankers) and fit the router |
| `b_tune` | 1,000 | choose the routing threshold Ï„ |
| `b_eval` | 2,000 | **all pre-registered tests, once** |
| `router_train` | 1,000 | refit exp13's hop router exactly (comparison only) |
| `dev_fresh` | 1,017 | descriptive only: **re-used** from exp13, where it informed this design |

## Router (fixed now)

- **Label per question:** g = hit@5(iterative) âˆ’ hit@5(one-shot) âˆˆ {âˆ’1, 0, 1}.
- **Model:** `HistGradientBoostingRegressor(max_iter=200, learning_rate=0.05, random_state=0)` predicting g. Fit on `b_train`.
- **Features**, all available after the one-shot pass:
  - question word count, commas, " of " count, "'s" count, wh-word count, capitalized words after the first
  - the one-shot model's sorted top-10 scores, the gaps between the top 6, and the softmax entropy of the top 10
- **Threshold Ï„:** on `b_tune`, from the 101 quantiles of the predicted g. Choose the Ï„ with the **highest cascade recall@5** subject to **mean passes â‰¤ 50**. Ties go to fewer routed questions.

## Pre-registered tests (b_eval, n = 2,000)

- **Gate:** if always-iterative minus one-shot recall@5 on `b_eval` is **< 0.02**, there is too little benefit to capture, and the primary is reported as **not testable**.
- **Primary:** benefit cascade vs one-shot, recall@5, McNemar exact, two-sided Î± = 0.05.
  - **Success** = the gate passes, the cascade is higher, p < 0.05, **and** mean passes â‰¤ 50.
  - **Minimum worthwhile effect:** +0.01 recall@5.
  - **Power:** exp10's train-distribution holdout showed iterative â‰  one-shot on about 19% of questions (unmatched models). If the cascade captures half of a +0.03 gain, about +0.015, then with about 6% discordant questions (120) the test has roughly 50% power. **The CI is the main evidence.**
- **Secondary:**
  - **a.** Benefit router vs exp13's hop router (refit exactly as in exp13), recall@5 McNemar. "Beats" = higher and p < 0.05.
  - **b.** Benefit cascade vs always-iterative: non-inferiority, unrounded 95% CI lower bound > âˆ’0.01.
- **Descriptive:**
  - an oracle benefit router (route iff g > 0): the upper bound
  - the AUC of predicted g for "g > 0"
  - routed fraction, passes, recall@k, by hops
  - the frozen router and Ï„ applied to `dev_fresh` (labelled as re-use)

**Claim ledger:** one primary (H12). The secondaries are labelled secondary.

## Compute

- **Colab T4.** About 7,000 questions Ã— (one-shot + iterative), roughly 50â€“60 min.
- **Inputs:**
  - A1 tokenizer from the Colab Drive (sha-checked)
  - MuSiQue from the official release: dev sha `15fa63â€¦bf3b`; the train subset of 8,000 rows (`b_*` âˆª `router_train`, LF) sha `4093c1â€¦c49b`
  - exp12 models and exp13 rankings from the Colab Drive
- The notebook is pinned to the registration commit and writes to a fresh output folder.

## Honest priors

- **Gate:** may fail. Train-distribution questions are mostly 2-hop, and exp13's train 4-hop stratum gained only +0.02.
- **Benefit is hard to predict:** g is sparse and noisy (about 12% "+1", 7% "âˆ’1" in exp10's holdout). An AUC near 0.6 would be typical, and the cascade gain could be within noise.
- **Even a null is informative:** it would say iteration's benefit is not predictable from one-shot signals, so the choice is all-or-nothing.
"""exp13 ranking (PROTOCOL.md): exp12's frozen GPU models, one-shot and iterative inference, on the fresh splits.

  python rank.py --split f4_dev --only iter_iter ctrl_oneshot
Writes RESULTS_DIR/rank_<split>.jsonl: one line per config with per-question orders, one-shot scores (top 10) and passes.
Resumable per config.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import hashlib
import json
import os
import time
from pathlib import Path

import torch
from transformers import AutoModelForSequenceClassification, AutoTokenizer

from data import encode, load, paragraphs_of, query

HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
MODELS = Path(os.environ.get("MODELS_DIR", HERE / "models"))  # exp12 models (Colab Drive)
A1 = Path(os.environ.get("A1_DIR", HERE.parent / "exp04_astra_edit" / "models" / "a1"))  # tokenizer only
DEV = "cuda" if torch.cuda.is_available() else "cpu"
STEPS = 4
CONFIGS = {"iter_iter": ("m1_iter_s0", True), "ctrl_oneshot": ("m1_ctrl", False),
           "iter_iter_s1": ("m1_iter_s1", True), "ctrl_oneshot_s1": ("m1_ctrl_s1", False),
           "iter_iter_s2": ("m1_iter_s2", True), "ctrl_oneshot_s2": ("m1_ctrl_s2", False),
           "iter_oneshot": ("m1_iter_s0", False), "ctrl_iter": ("m1_ctrl", True)}  # 2x2 on 4-hop (descriptive)


@torch.no_grad()
def rank_all(model, tok, rows, iterative):
    """exp09 inference: one-shot sort, or 4 greedy steps with the tail ordered by the step-3 scores."""
    def score(q, ps):
        return model(**encode(tok, [q] * len(ps), ps).to(DEV)).logits.float().cpu().squeeze(-1).reshape(-1).tolist()

    out = []
    for r in rows:
        _, paras = paragraphs_of(r)
        rest, order, passes, top, qlen = list(range(len(paras))), [], 0, None, []
        for _ in range(STEPS if iterative else 1):
            q = query(r["question"], [paras[i] for i in order]); qlen.append(len(tok(q)["input_ids"]))
            s = score(q, [paras[i] for i in rest])
            passes += len(rest)
            ranked = sorted(zip(s, rest), key=lambda z: -z[0])
            if not iterative:
                order, top = [i for _, i in ranked], [round(v, 4) for v, _ in ranked[:10]]
                break
            order.append(ranked[0][1]); rest = [i for _, i in ranked[1:]]
        order = order + (rest if iterative else [])
        assert sorted(order) == list(range(len(paras))), "order is not a full permutation"
        out.append({"order": order, "passes": passes, "top10": top, "max_query_tokens": max(qlen)})
    return out


def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("--split", required=True)
    ap.add_argument("--only", nargs="*", default=list(CONFIGS))
    args = ap.parse_args()
    assert DEV == "cuda" or os.environ.get("ALLOW_CPU"), "PROTOCOL: Colab GPU"
    tok = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(str(A1))
    rows = load(args.split)
    out = Path(os.environ.get("RESULTS_DIR", HERE / "results")) / f"rank_{args.split}.jsonl"
    done = {(j["config"], j["sha256"]) for j in map(json.loads, out.read_text().splitlines())} if out.exists() else set()
    for name in args.only:
        tag, iterative = CONFIGS[name]
        sha = hashlib.sha256((MODELS / tag / "model" / "model.safetensors").read_bytes()).hexdigest()
        if (name, sha) in done:
            continue
        assert name not in {c for c, _ in done}, f"{name} exists with a different model hash"
        t0 = time.time()
        model = AutoModelForSequenceClassification.from_pretrained(str(MODELS / tag / "model")).eval().to(DEV)
        res = rank_all(model, tok, rows, iterative)
        with out.open("a") as fh:
            fh.write(json.dumps({"config": name, "model": tag, "iterative": iterative, "ids": [r["id"] for r in rows],
                                 "orders": [x["order"] for x in res], "passes": [x["passes"] for x in res],
                                 "top10": [x["top10"] for x in res], "max_query_tokens": [x["max_query_tokens"] for x in res],
                                 "sha256": sha, "device": DEV}) + "\n")
        print(args.split, name, round(time.time() - t0), "s", flush=True)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
"""exp14 pre-registered analysis (PROTOCOL.md). Written before any exp14 ranking was run.

  python analyze.py      # RESULTS_DIR: rank_{b_train,b_tune,b_eval,router_train}.jsonl (+ optional rank_dev_fresh.jsonl)
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
import os
from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np
from scipy.stats import binomtest
from sklearn.ensemble import HistGradientBoostingRegressor
from sklearn.linear_model import LogisticRegression
from sklearn.metrics import roc_auc_score
from sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler

from data import gold_mask, hops, load

HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
RES = Path(os.environ.get("RESULTS_DIR", HERE / "results"))
B, KS, MAX_PASSES = 5000, (2, 3, 4, 5, 10, 15), 50.0
WH = ("who", "what", "which", "where", "when", "how")


def ranks(split):
    rows = load(split); out = {}
    for line in (RES / f"rank_{split}.jsonl").read_text().splitlines():
        j = json.loads(line)
        if j["config"] not in ("iter_iter", "ctrl_oneshot"):
            continue
        assert j["ids"] == [r["id"] for r in rows], f"ids differ: {split}"
        assert j["config"] not in out, f"duplicate {split} {j['config']}"
        assert all(sorted(o) == list(range(len(r["paragraphs"]))) for o, r in zip(j["orders"], rows))
        out[j["config"]] = j
    return rows, out


def hits(rows, orders, k=5):
    return np.array([int(np.array(gold_mask(r), bool)[o[:k]].sum() == sum(gold_mask(r))) for r, o in zip(rows, orders)])


def mcnemar(x, y):
    b, c = int(((x == 1) & (y == 0)).sum()), int(((x == 0) & (y == 1)).sum())
    return {"only_x": b, "only_y": c, "p": float(binomtest(b, b + c, 0.5).pvalue) if b + c else 1.0}


def boot(d, rng):
    n = len(d); bs = [d[rng.integers(0, n, n)].mean() for _ in range(B)]
    return [float(np.percentile(bs, 2.5)), float(np.percentile(bs, 97.5))]


def feats_benefit(rows, one):
    """PROTOCOL: question text features + the one-shot model's score profile (available after the one-shot pass)."""
    F = []
    for r, t in zip(rows, one["top10"]):
        q = r["question"]; w = q.lower().split(); s = np.array(t, float)
        p = np.exp(s - s.max()); p /= p.sum()
        F.append([len(w), q.count(","), q.lower().count(" of "), q.count("'s"), sum(x in WH for x in w),
                  sum(x[:1].isupper() for x in q.split()[1:]), *s, *(s[:5] - s[1:6]), float(-(p * np.log(p)).sum())])
    return np.array(F, float)


def feats_hop(rows, one):  # exp13's router features (comparison)
    return np.array([[len(r["question"].split()), r["question"].count(",")] + t for r, t in zip(rows, one["top10"])], float)


def cascade(rows, R, route):
    one, itr = R["ctrl_oneshot"], R["iter_iter"]
    orders = [itr["orders"][i] if route[i] else one["orders"][i] for i in range(len(rows))]
    passes = np.array(one["passes"]) + np.where(route, np.array(itr["passes"]), 0)
    return orders, float(passes.mean())


def main():
    rng, rep = np.random.default_rng(0), {}
    D = {sp: ranks(sp) for sp in ("b_train", "b_tune", "b_eval", "router_train")}
    gain = {sp: hits(rows, R["iter_iter"]["orders"]) - hits(rows, R["ctrl_oneshot"]["orders"]) for sp, (rows, R) in D.items() if sp != "router_train"}

    # Benefit router: gradient-boosted regression of g = iter_hit5 - oneshot_hit5, fit on b_train
    Xtr = feats_benefit(*(D["b_train"][0], D["b_train"][1]["ctrl_oneshot"]))
    reg = HistGradientBoostingRegressor(max_iter=200, learning_rate=0.05, random_state=0).fit(Xtr, gain["b_train"])
    pred = {sp: reg.predict(feats_benefit(D[sp][0], D[sp][1]["ctrl_oneshot"])) for sp in ("b_tune", "b_eval")}

    # tau on b_tune: max cascade recall@5 with mean passes <= 50 (ties -> fewer routed)
    rows, R = D["b_tune"]; best = None
    for tau in np.unique(np.quantile(pred["b_tune"], np.linspace(0, 1, 101))):
        route = pred["b_tune"] >= tau; orders, ps = cascade(rows, R, route)
        if ps <= MAX_PASSES:
            key = (hits(rows, orders).mean(), -route.mean())
            if best is None or key > best[0]:
                best = (key, float(tau))
    tau = best[1] if best else float("inf")
    rep["router"] = {"tau": tau, "b_tune_recall5": round(best[0][0], 4) if best else None}

    # exp13-style hop router (comparison), refit exactly as exp13 on router_train
    rt_rows, rt = D["router_train"]
    Fh = feats_hop(rt_rows, rt["ctrl_oneshot"]); yh = np.array([int(hops(r) == 4) for r in rt_rows])
    sc = StandardScaler().fit(Fh); lr = LogisticRegression(max_iter=5000, class_weight="balanced").fit(sc.transform(Fh), yh)

    # Evaluation on b_eval
    rows, R = D["b_eval"]; hp = np.array([hops(r) for r in rows])
    o, i = hits(rows, R["ctrl_oneshot"]["orders"]), hits(rows, R["iter_iter"]["orders"])
    routes = {"benefit": pred["b_eval"] >= tau,
              "hop_exp13": lr.predict_proba(sc.transform(feats_hop(rows, R["ctrl_oneshot"])))[:, 1] >= 0.5,
              "oracle_benefit": gain["b_eval"] > 0}
    rep["b_eval"] = {"n": len(rows), "oneshot_recall5": round(float(o.mean()), 4), "iterative_recall5": round(float(i.mean()), 4),
                     "iterative_gain": round(float((i - o).mean()), 4), "iterative_gain_ci95": boot(i - o, rng),
                     "benefit_auc": round(float(roc_auc_score(gain["b_eval"] > 0, pred["b_eval"])), 4) if (gain["b_eval"] > 0).any() else None}
    H = {}
    for name, route in routes.items():
        orders, ps = cascade(rows, R, route); H[name] = hits(rows, orders)
        rep["b_eval"][name] = {"recall5": round(float(H[name].mean()), 4), "passes": round(ps, 1), "routed": round(float(route.mean()), 3),
                               "recall_at_k": {k: round(float(hits(rows, orders, k).mean()), 4) for k in KS},
                               "by_hops_recall5": {int(h): round(float(H[name][hp == h].mean()), 3) for h in (2, 3, 4)}}
    c = H["benefit"]
    gate = float((i - o).mean()) >= 0.02
    pr = {"gate_iterative_gain_ge_0.02": gate, "diff": round(float((c - o).mean()), 4), "diff_ci95": boot(c - o, rng), **mcnemar(c, o),
          "passes": rep["b_eval"]["benefit"]["passes"]}
    pr["success"] = bool(gate and c.mean() > o.mean() and pr["p"] < 0.05 and pr["passes"] <= MAX_PASSES)
    pr["status"] = "not testable (iterative gain < 0.02)" if not gate else ("holds" if pr["success"] else "fails")
    rep["primary"] = pr
    h = H["hop_exp13"]
    rep["secondary"] = {"benefit_vs_hop_router": {"diff": round(float((c - h).mean()), 4), "diff_ci95": boot(c - h, rng), **mcnemar(c, h)},
                        "benefit_vs_iterative": {"diff": round(float((c - i).mean()), 4), "diff_ci95": boot(c - i, rng)}}
    s = rep["secondary"]
    s["beats_hop_router"] = bool(c.mean() > h.mean() and s["benefit_vs_hop_router"]["p"] < 0.05)
    s["noninferior_to_iterative"] = bool(s["benefit_vs_iterative"]["diff_ci95"][0] > -0.01)

    # Descriptive: the same frozen router + tau on dev_fresh (re-used set from exp13; labelled re-use)
    if (RES / "rank_dev_fresh.jsonl").exists():
        rows, R = ranks("dev_fresh"); route = reg.predict(feats_benefit(rows, R["ctrl_oneshot"])) >= tau
        orders, ps = cascade(rows, R, route)
        o2, i2, c2 = (hits(rows, R["ctrl_oneshot"]["orders"]), hits(rows, R["iter_iter"]["orders"]), hits(rows, orders))
        rep["dev_fresh_reuse"] = {"oneshot": round(float(o2.mean()), 4), "iterative": round(float(i2.mean()), 4),
                                  "benefit_cascade": round(float(c2.mean()), 4), "passes": round(ps, 1), "routed": round(float(route.mean()), 3),
                                  "cascade_vs_oneshot": mcnemar(c2, o2)}
    (RES / "results.json").write_text(json.dumps(rep, indent=1))
    print(json.dumps(rep, indent=1))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

("markdown", "## Rank (frozen exp12 models; exp13's router_train and dev_fresh rankings reused), then the pre-registered analysis"),
("code", """import shutil
for f in ('rank_router_train.jsonl', 'rank_dev_fresh.jsonl'):
    if not os.path.exists(f'{OUT}/results/{f}'): shutil.copy(f'{E13}/{f}', f'{OUT}/results/{f}')
for sp in ('b_train', 'b_tune', 'b_eval'):

!python analyze.py"""),
]
nb = {"cells": [{"cell_type": t, "metadata": {}, "source": s.splitlines(True), **({"outputs": [], "execution_count": None} if t == "code" else {})}
                for t, s in cells],
      "metadata": {"accelerator": "GPU", "colab": {"gpuType": "T4"}, "kernelspec": {"name": "python3", "display_name": "Python 3"}},
      "nbformat": 4, "nbformat_minor": 5}
txt = json.dumps(nb, indent=1).replace("__PIN__", PIN)

"""MuSiQue-Ans loading, paragraph format and prompt (copied from exp08; data files read from ../exp08_musique/data)."""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
from functools import lru_cache
from pathlib import Path

HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
FILES = {"dev": "musique_ans_v1.0_dev.jsonl", "pool": "musique_ans_v1.0_train.jsonl"}
SPLITS = {"b_train": "pool", "b_tune": "pool", "b_eval": "pool", "router_train": "pool", "dev_fresh": "dev"}  # split -> source file


@lru_cache(maxsize=None)
def _file(src: str) -> dict:
    with open(HERE.parent / "exp08_musique" / "data" / FILES[src], encoding="utf-8") as fh:
        return {r["id"]: r for r in map(json.loads, fh)}


def load(split: str) -> list[dict]:
    ids = json.loads((HERE / "split_ids.json").read_text())[split]
    rows = _file(SPLITS[split])
    return [rows[i] for i in ids]


def paragraphs_of(row: dict) -> tuple[list[str], list[str]]:
    """'<title>. <text>' as in exp02."""
    ps = sorted(row["paragraphs"], key=lambda p: p["idx"])
    titles = [p["title"].strip() for p in ps]
    return titles, [f"{t}. {p['paragraph_text'].strip()}" for t, p in zip(titles, ps)]


def gold_mask(row: dict) -> list[int]:
    return [int(p["is_supporting"]) for p in sorted(row["paragraphs"], key=lambda p: p["idx"])]


def hops(row: dict) -> int:
    return len(row["question_decomposition"])


def build_retrieval_prompt(question: str, paragraphs: list[str]) -> str:
    """exp02/BRACIS prompt; only change: 'the HotpotQA question' -> 'the question'."""
    context_str = "\n\n".join([f"[{i + 1}] {p}" for i, p in enumerate(paragraphs)])
    return f"""Answer the question using only the provided context.

Rules:
- Output only the final answer.
- If the answer is yes, no or noanswer, output exactly: yes or no or noanswer.
- Otherwise output a short span or entity name only.
- Do not include any explanation.
- Do not repeat the question.
- If multiple positions/titles are mentioned, output only the one that
  directly answers the question.

Context:
{context_str}

Question: {question}

Answer:"""


_ENC = None


def n_tokens(text: str) -> int:
    global _ENC
    if _ENC is None:
        import tiktoken

        _ENC = tiktoken.get_encoding("o200k_base")
    return len(_ENC.encode(text))


def chain(row: dict) -> list[int]:
    """Gold paragraph positions (in paragraphs_of order) in decomposition (hop) order."""
    pos = {p["idx"]: i for i, p in enumerate(sorted(row["paragraphs"], key=lambda p: p["idx"]))}
    return [pos[d["paragraph_support_idx"]] for d in row["question_decomposition"]]


def query(question: str, selected: list[str]) -> str:
    """Question + selected paragraphs, most recent first (truncation cuts the oldest)."""
    return " [SEP] ".join([question, *reversed(selected)])


def encode(tok, qs, ps, max_length=512):
    """Truncate the query side only (PROTOCOL); if a paragraph alone exceeds max_length, fall back to longest_first."""
    try:
        return tok(qs, ps, truncation="only_first", max_length=max_length, padding=True, return_tensors="pt")
    except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - tokenizers raises a bare Exception for this case
        return tok(qs, ps, truncation="longest_first", max_length=max_length, padding=True, return_tensors="pt")

# exp13: 4-hop confirmation and a one-shot â†’ iterative cascade

Pre-registered in [PROTOCOL.md](PROTOCOL.md) (commit d071b74, revised after an adversarial Codex pre-run review).
- **Models:** exp12's frozen GPU models. M1-iter and M1-ctrl were trained on identical pairs.
- **Data:** fresh questions never used before (seed-13 splits).
- **Compute:** Colab T4, code pinned to d071b74. The analysis is pre-written (`analyze.py`).
- **Results:** [`results/results.json`](results/results.json), with per-question hits and router decisions in [`results/hits_compact.json`](results/hits_compact.json). Both were checked locally: the primary recomputed from the hits matches exactly.

## Bottom line

1. **Primary confirmed: on fresh 4-hop chains, conditioning-trained iteration beats matched one-shot ranking.** n = 566, recall@5 **0.500 vs 0.445, +0.055 [+0.007, +0.102]**, 117 vs 86 discordant questions, **p = 0.035**. Both strata are positive, so the success rule is met.
   - **The strata differ a lot:**
     - dev 4-hop (n = 166): **+0.139** (p = 0.006)
     - train 4-hop (n = 400): **+0.020** (n.s.)
   - **Seeds:** +0.055 / +0.044 / +0.016, all positive.
   - **Size:** the effect is at the pre-registered minimum worthwhile effect (+0.05), not the +0.19 seen after the fact in exp12, as the stated prior expected.
2. **The cascade fails both secondaries.**
   - **Router:** routes 29% of questions; 4-hop recall 0.76, precision 0.43.
   - **Cascade:** recall@5 0.702 at 41.5 passes, against one-shot 0.693 at 20 and always-iterative 0.745 at 74.
     - vs one-shot: **+0.009 [âˆ’0.010, +0.027]**, p = 0.39 âœ—
     - vs iterative: **âˆ’0.043 [âˆ’0.068, âˆ’0.020]**, not non-inferior âœ—
   - **Even the oracle cascade loses:** routing exactly the 4-hop questions gives 0.716 at 32 passes. Iteration also helps 3-hop chains on these fresh questions, so a 4-hop-only router leaves gain on the table.
3. **Exploratory (not pre-registered, uncorrected): on all 1,017 fresh dev questions, iteration helps chains of 3 or more hops.**

   | Hops | Iterative | One-shot | Discordant | p |
   |---|---|---|---|---|
   | all | **0.745** | 0.693 | 151 / 98 | 0.0009 |
   | 2 | 0.822 | 0.813 | 51 / 46 | 0.68 |
   | 3 | **0.724** | 0.646 | 56 / 31 | 0.010 |
   | 4 | **0.542** | 0.404 | 44 / 21 | 0.006 |

   This is larger than exp12's overall +0.04 on its 400 test questions (n.s.). Taken together, the evidence points to a real but modest gain from iteration that comes entirely from â‰¥ 3-hop chains. A pooled analysis would need to be pre-registered.
4. **The router picks questions that look hard, not questions where iteration helps.** It routed 45% of 3-hop questions, yet the cascade's 3-hop recall (0.640) equals one-shot's (0.646). Score shape and question length predict hop count, but not iteration benefit.

## 2Ã—2 on fresh 4-hop chains (recall@5)

| | f4_dev: one-shot | f4_dev: iterative | f4_train: one-shot | f4_train: iterative |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M1-iter | 0.337 | **0.542** | 0.393 | **0.483** |
| M1-ctrl | **0.404** | 0.169 | **0.463** | 0.118 |

- Each model is best in the mode it was trained for, as in exp12.
- 17â€“19% of the iterative 4-hop questions reach the 512-token limit at the last step, so the oldest picked paragraph is truncated.

## What this changes

- **H6-4hop:** confirmed (C). The effect is modest (+0.055) and heterogeneous across sources.
- **Cascade (H11):** N. A 4-hop router built from one-shot score features does not make a useful cascade.
- **For the thesis:**
  - *Research:* the conditioning skill (a rank-2 change, exp12) gives a real gain on chains of 3 or more hops, about +0.05 to +0.14 recall@5, and nothing on 2-hop chains.
  - *Practical:* the obvious cascade fails. The open problem is predicting *where iteration helps*, not predicting hop count. A router trained on that benefit directly, labelled by running both rankers on a training split, is the next candidate.

## Deviations

- **The router's cascade behavior was partly seen in a smoke test** before registration: question-length features with random scores gave 4-hop recall 0.75 and precision 0.41. This is disclosed in PROTOCOL, and nothing was changed.
- **Colab reset its runtime once** while waiting for the Drive authorization, before any ranking. The run started from scratch in a fresh output folder.

### experiments/exp14_benefit_router/PROTOCOL.md
   1: # exp14 protocol: a cascade with a router trained on iteration benefit (pre-registered 2026-11-15)
   2: 
   3: ## Why
   4: 
   5: exp13 established three things:
   6: - **4-hop gain is real:** conditioning-trained iteration beats matched one-shot ranking on fresh 4-hop chains (+0.055, p = 0.035).
   7: - **The 4-hop cascade failed:** its router predicts *hop count*, not *where iteration helps*.
   8: - **3-hop gains too (exploratory):** on fresh dev questions, iteration also helps 3-hop chains.
   9: 
  10: **Question:** can a cheap router, trained directly on per-question benefit, capture iteration's gain at a fraction of its cost?
  11: 
  12: ## Models (frozen, exp12 GPU seed 0)
  13: 
  14: - **One-shot:** M1-ctrl (`m1_ctrl`), 20 passes per question.
  15: - **Iterative:** M1-iter (`m1_iter_s0`), about 74 passes.
  16: - **The cascade:**
  17:   - every question gets the one-shot pass
  18:   - routed questions also get the iterative pass, about 20 + 74
  19:   - routed questions use the iterative ranking
  20: - exp13's rankings of `router_train` and `dev_fresh` were made with these same models (sha recorded) and are reused.
  21: 
  22: ## Data (`split_ids.json`, fixed before any scoring; seed 14)
  23: 
  24: Drawn from the 13,538 MuSiQue-Ans train questions **never used** in exp08â€“exp13 (natural hop mix: about 74% 2-hop, 22% 3-hop, 3â€“4% 4-hop):
  25: 
  26: | Split | n | Use |
  27: |---|---|---|
  28: | `b_train` | 4,000 | label benefit (run both rankers) and fit the router |
  29: | `b_tune` | 1,000 | choose the routing threshold Ï„ |
  30: | `b_eval` | 2,000 | **all pre-registered tests, once** |
  31: | `router_train` | 1,000 | refit exp13's hop router exactly (comparison only) |
  32: | `dev_fresh` | 1,017 | descriptive only: **re-used** from exp13, where it informed this design |
  33: 
  34: ## Router (fixed now)
  35: 
  36: - **Label per question:** g = hit@5(iterative) âˆ’ hit@5(one-shot) âˆˆ {âˆ’1, 0, 1}.
  37: - **Model:** `HistGradientBoostingRegressor(max_iter=200, learning_rate=0.05, random_state=0)` predicting g. Fit on `b_train`.
  38: - **Features**, all available after the one-shot pass:
  39:   - question word count, commas, " of " count, "'s" count, wh-word count, capitalized words after the first
  40:   - the one-shot model's sorted top-10 scores, the gaps between the top 6, and the softmax entropy of the top 10
  41: - **Threshold Ï„:** on `b_tune`, from the 101 quantiles of the predicted g. Choose the Ï„ with the **highest cascade recall@5** subject to **mean passes â‰¤ 50**. Ties go to fewer routed questions.
  42: 
  43: ## Pre-registered tests (b_eval, n = 2,000)
  44: 
  45: - **Gate:** if always-iterative minus one-shot recall@5 on `b_eval` is **< 0.02**, there is too little benefit to capture, and the primary is reported as **not testable**.
  46: - **Primary:** benefit cascade vs one-shot, recall@5, McNemar exact, two-sided Î± = 0.05.
  47:   - **Success** = the gate passes, the cascade is higher, p < 0.05, **and** mean passes â‰¤ 50.
  48:   - **Minimum worthwhile effect:** +0.01 recall@5.
  49:   - **Power:** exp10's train-distribution holdout showed iterative â‰  one-shot on about 19% of questions (unmatched models). If the cascade captures half of a +0.03 gain, about +0.015, then with about 6% discordant questions (120) the test has roughly 50% power. **The CI is the main evidence.**
  50: - **Secondary:**
  51:   - **a.** Benefit router vs exp13's hop router (refit exactly as in exp13), recall@5 McNemar. "Beats" = higher and p < 0.05.
  52:   - **b.** Benefit cascade vs always-iterative: non-inferiority, unrounded 95% CI lower bound > âˆ’0.01.
  53: - **Descriptive:**
  54:   - an oracle benefit router (route iff g > 0): the upper bound
  55:   - the AUC of predicted g for "g > 0"
  56:   - routed fraction, passes, recall@k, by hops
  57:   - the frozen router and Ï„ applied to `dev_fresh` (labelled as re-use)
  58: 
  59: **Claim ledger:** one primary (H12). The secondaries are labelled secondary.
  60: 
  61: ## Compute
  62: 
  63: - **Colab T4.** About 7,000 questions Ã— (one-shot + iterative), roughly 50â€“60 min.
  64: - **Inputs:**
  65:   - A1 tokenizer from the Colab Drive (sha-checked)
  66:   - MuSiQue from the official release: dev sha `15fa63â€¦bf3b`; the train subset of 8,000 rows (`b_*` âˆª `router_train`, LF) sha `4093c1â€¦c49b`
  67:   - exp12 models and exp13 rankings from the Colab Drive
  68: - The notebook is pinned to the registration commit and writes to a fresh output folder.
  69: 
  70: ## Honest priors
  71: 
  72: - **Gate:** may fail. Train-distribution questions are mostly 2-hop, and exp13's train 4-hop stratum gained only +0.02.
  73: - **Benefit is hard to predict:** g is sparse and noisy (about 12% "+1", 7% "âˆ’1" in exp10's holdout). An AUC near 0.6 would be typical, and the cascade gain could be within noise.
  74: - **Even a null is informative:** it would say iteration's benefit is not predictable from one-shot signals, so the choice is all-or-nothing.
### experiments/exp14_benefit_router/rank.py
   1: """exp13 ranking (PROTOCOL.md): exp12's frozen GPU models, one-shot and iterative inference, on the fresh splits.
   2: 
   3:   python rank.py --split f4_dev --only iter_iter ctrl_oneshot
   4: Writes RESULTS_DIR/rank_<split>.jsonl: one line per config with per-question orders, one-shot scores (top 10) and passes.
   5: Resumable per config.
   6: """
   7: 
   8: from __future__ import annotations
   9: 
  10: import argparse
  11: import hashlib
  12: import json
  13: import os
  14: import time
  15: from pathlib import Path
  16: 
  17: import torch
  18: from transformers import AutoModelForSequenceClassification, AutoTokenizer
  19: 
  20: from data import encode, load, paragraphs_of, query
  21: 
  22: HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
  23: MODELS = Path(os.environ.get("MODELS_DIR", HERE / "models"))  # exp12 models (Colab Drive)
  24: A1 = Path(os.environ.get("A1_DIR", HERE.parent / "exp04_astra_edit" / "models" / "a1"))  # tokenizer only
  25: DEV = "cuda" if torch.cuda.is_available() else "cpu"
  26: STEPS = 4
  27: CONFIGS = {"iter_iter": ("m1_iter_s0", True), "ctrl_oneshot": ("m1_ctrl", False),
  28:            "iter_iter_s1": ("m1_iter_s1", True), "ctrl_oneshot_s1": ("m1_ctrl_s1", False),
  29:            "iter_iter_s2": ("m1_iter_s2", True), "ctrl_oneshot_s2": ("m1_ctrl_s2", False),
  30:            "iter_oneshot": ("m1_iter_s0", False), "ctrl_iter": ("m1_ctrl", True)}  # 2x2 on 4-hop (descriptive)
  31: 
  32: 
  33: @torch.no_grad()
  34: def rank_all(model, tok, rows, iterative):
  35:     """exp09 inference: one-shot sort, or 4 greedy steps with the tail ordered by the step-3 scores."""
  36:     def score(q, ps):
  37:         return model(**encode(tok, [q] * len(ps), ps).to(DEV)).logits.float().cpu().squeeze(-1).reshape(-1).tolist()
  38: 
  39:     out = []
  40:     for r in rows:
  41:         _, paras = paragraphs_of(r)
  42:         rest, order, passes, top, qlen = list(range(len(paras))), [], 0, None, []
  43:         for _ in range(STEPS if iterative else 1):
  44:             q = query(r["question"], [paras[i] for i in order]); qlen.append(len(tok(q)["input_ids"]))
  45:             s = score(q, [paras[i] for i in rest])
  46:             passes += len(rest)
  47:             ranked = sorted(zip(s, rest), key=lambda z: -z[0])
  48:             if not iterative:
  49:                 order, top = [i for _, i in ranked], [round(v, 4) for v, _ in ranked[:10]]
  50:                 break
  51:             order.append(ranked[0][1]); rest = [i for _, i in ranked[1:]]
  52:         order = order + (rest if iterative else [])
  53:         assert sorted(order) == list(range(len(paras))), "order is not a full permutation"
  54:         out.append({"order": order, "passes": passes, "top10": top, "max_query_tokens": max(qlen)})
  55:     return out
  56: 
  57: 
  58: def main():
  59:     ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
  60:     ap.add_argument("--split", required=True)
  61:     ap.add_argument("--only", nargs="*", default=list(CONFIGS))
  62:     args = ap.parse_args()
  63:     assert DEV == "cuda" or os.environ.get("ALLOW_CPU"), "PROTOCOL: Colab GPU"
  64:     tok = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(str(A1))
  65:     rows = load(args.split)
  66:     out = Path(os.environ.get("RESULTS_DIR", HERE / "results")) / f"rank_{args.split}.jsonl"
  67:     done = {(j["config"], j["sha256"]) for j in map(json.loads, out.read_text().splitlines())} if out.exists() else set()
  68:     for name in args.only:
  69:         tag, iterative = CONFIGS[name]
  70:         sha = hashlib.sha256((MODELS / tag / "model" / "model.safetensors").read_bytes()).hexdigest()
  71:         if (name, sha) in done:
  72:             continue
  73:         assert name not in {c for c, _ in done}, f"{name} exists with a different model hash"
  74:         t0 = time.time()
  75:         model = AutoModelForSequenceClassification.from_pretrained(str(MODELS / tag / "model")).eval().to(DEV)
  76:         res = rank_all(model, tok, rows, iterative)
  77:         with out.open("a") as fh:
  78:             fh.write(json.dumps({"config": name, "model": tag, "iterative": iterative, "ids": [r["id"] for r in rows],
  79:                                  "orders": [x["order"] for x in res], "passes": [x["passes"] for x in res],
  80:                                  "top10": [x["top10"] for x in res], "max_query_tokens": [x["max_query_tokens"] for x in res],
  81:                                  "sha256": sha, "device": DEV}) + "\n")
  82:         print(args.split, name, round(time.time() - t0), "s", flush=True)
  83: 
  84: 
  85: if __name__ == "__main__":
  86:     main()
### experiments/exp14_benefit_router/analyze.py
   1: """exp14 pre-registered analysis (PROTOCOL.md). Written before any exp14 ranking was run.
   2: 
   3:   python analyze.py      # RESULTS_DIR: rank_{b_train,b_tune,b_eval,router_train}.jsonl (+ optional rank_dev_fresh.jsonl)
   4: """
   5: 
   6: from __future__ import annotations
   7: 
   8: import json
   9: import os
  10: from pathlib import Path
  11: 
  12: import numpy as np
  13: from scipy.stats import binomtest
  14: from sklearn.ensemble import HistGradientBoostingRegressor
  15: from sklearn.linear_model import LogisticRegression
  16: from sklearn.metrics import roc_auc_score
  17: from sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler
  18: 
  19: from data import gold_mask, hops, load
  20: 
  21: HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
  22: RES = Path(os.environ.get("RESULTS_DIR", HERE / "results"))
  23: B, KS, MAX_PASSES = 5000, (2, 3, 4, 5, 10, 15), 50.0
  24: WH = ("who", "what", "which", "where", "when", "how")
  25: 
  26: 
  27: def ranks(split):
  28:     rows = load(split); out = {}
  29:     for line in (RES / f"rank_{split}.jsonl").read_text().splitlines():
  30:         j = json.loads(line)
  31:         if j["config"] not in ("iter_iter", "ctrl_oneshot"):
  32:             continue
  33:         assert j["ids"] == [r["id"] for r in rows], f"ids differ: {split}"
  34:         assert j["config"] not in out, f"duplicate {split} {j['config']}"
  35:         assert all(sorted(o) == list(range(len(r["paragraphs"]))) for o, r in zip(j["orders"], rows))
  36:         out[j["config"]] = j
  37:     return rows, out
  38: 
  39: 
  40: def hits(rows, orders, k=5):
  41:     return np.array([int(np.array(gold_mask(r), bool)[o[:k]].sum() == sum(gold_mask(r))) for r, o in zip(rows, orders)])
  42: 
  43: 
  44: def mcnemar(x, y):
  45:     b, c = int(((x == 1) & (y == 0)).sum()), int(((x == 0) & (y == 1)).sum())
  46:     return {"only_x": b, "only_y": c, "p": float(binomtest(b, b + c, 0.5).pvalue) if b + c else 1.0}
  47: 
  48: 
  49: def boot(d, rng):
  50:     n = len(d); bs = [d[rng.integers(0, n, n)].mean() for _ in range(B)]
  51:     return [float(np.percentile(bs, 2.5)), float(np.percentile(bs, 97.5))]
  52: 
  53: 
  54: def feats_benefit(rows, one):
  55:     """PROTOCOL: question text features + the one-shot model's score profile (available after the one-shot pass)."""
  56:     F = []
  57:     for r, t in zip(rows, one["top10"]):
  58:         q = r["question"]; w = q.lower().split(); s = np.array(t, float)
  59:         p = np.exp(s - s.max()); p /= p.sum()
  60:         F.append([len(w), q.count(","), q.lower().count(" of "), q.count("'s"), sum(x in WH for x in w),
  61:                   sum(x[:1].isupper() for x in q.split()[1:]), *s, *(s[:5] - s[1:6]), float(-(p * np.log(p)).sum())])
  62:     return np.array(F, float)
  63: 
  64: 
  65: def feats_hop(rows, one):  # exp13's router features (comparison)
  66:     return np.array([[len(r["question"].split()), r["question"].count(",")] + t for r, t in zip(rows, one["top10"])], float)
  67: 
  68: 
  69: def cascade(rows, R, route):
  70:     one, itr = R["ctrl_oneshot"], R["iter_iter"]
  71:     orders = [itr["orders"][i] if route[i] else one["orders"][i] for i in range(len(rows))]
  72:     passes = np.array(one["passes"]) + np.where(route, np.array(itr["passes"]), 0)
  73:     return orders, float(passes.mean())
  74: 
  75: 
  76: def main():
  77:     rng, rep = np.random.default_rng(0), {}
  78:     D = {sp: ranks(sp) for sp in ("b_train", "b_tune", "b_eval", "router_train")}
  79:     gain = {sp: hits(rows, R["iter_iter"]["orders"]) - hits(rows, R["ctrl_oneshot"]["orders"]) for sp, (rows, R) in D.items() if sp != "router_train"}
  80: 
  81:     # Benefit router: gradient-boosted regression of g = iter_hit5 - oneshot_hit5, fit on b_train
  82:     Xtr = feats_benefit(*(D["b_train"][0], D["b_train"][1]["ctrl_oneshot"]))
  83:     reg = HistGradientBoostingRegressor(max_iter=200, learning_rate=0.05, random_state=0).fit(Xtr, gain["b_train"])
  84:     pred = {sp: reg.predict(feats_benefit(D[sp][0], D[sp][1]["ctrl_oneshot"])) for sp in ("b_tune", "b_eval")}
  85: 
  86:     # tau on b_tune: max cascade recall@5 with mean passes <= 50 (ties -> fewer routed)
  87:     rows, R = D["b_tune"]; best = None
  88:     for tau in np.unique(np.quantile(pred["b_tune"], np.linspace(0, 1, 101))):
  89:         route = pred["b_tune"] >= tau; orders, ps = cascade(rows, R, route)
  90:         if ps <= MAX_PASSES:
  91:             key = (hits(rows, orders).mean(), -route.mean())
  92:             if best is None or key > best[0]:
  93:                 best = (key, float(tau))
  94:     tau = best[1] if best else float("inf")
  95:     rep["router"] = {"tau": tau, "b_tune_recall5": round(best[0][0], 4) if best else None}
  96: 
  97:     # exp13-style hop router (comparison), refit exactly as exp13 on router_train
  98:     rt_rows, rt = D["router_train"]
  99:     Fh = feats_hop(rt_rows, rt["ctrl_oneshot"]); yh = np.array([int(hops(r) == 4) for r in rt_rows])
 100:     sc = StandardScaler().fit(Fh); lr = LogisticRegression(max_iter=5000, class_weight="balanced").fit(sc.transform(Fh), yh)
 101: 
 102:     # Evaluation on b_eval
 103:     rows, R = D["b_eval"]; hp = np.array([hops(r) for r in rows])
 104:     o, i = hits(rows, R["ctrl_oneshot"]["orders"]), hits(rows, R["iter_iter"]["orders"])
 105:     routes = {"benefit": pred["b_eval"] >= tau,
 106:               "hop_exp13": lr.predict_proba(sc.transform(feats_hop(rows, R["ctrl_oneshot"])))[:, 1] >= 0.5,
 107:               "oracle_benefit": gain["b_eval"] > 0}
 108:     rep["b_eval"] = {"n": len(rows), "oneshot_recall5": round(float(o.mean()), 4), "iterative_recall5": round(float(i.mean()), 4),
 109:                      "iterative_gain": round(float((i - o).mean()), 4), "iterative_gain_ci95": boot(i - o, rng),
 110:                      "benefit_auc": round(float(roc_auc_score(gain["b_eval"] > 0, pred["b_eval"])), 4) if (gain["b_eval"] > 0).any() else None}
 111:     H = {}
 112:     for name, route in routes.items():
 113:         orders, ps = cascade(rows, R, route); H[name] = hits(rows, orders)
 114:         rep["b_eval"][name] = {"recall5": round(float(H[name].mean()), 4), "passes": round(ps, 1), "routed": round(float(route.mean()), 3),
 115:                                "recall_at_k": {k: round(float(hits(rows, orders, k).mean()), 4) for k in KS},
 116:                                "by_hops_recall5": {int(h): round(float(H[name][hp == h].mean()), 3) for h in (2, 3, 4)}}
 117:     c = H["benefit"]
 118:     gate = float((i - o).mean()) >= 0.02
 119:     pr = {"gate_iterative_gain_ge_0.02": gate, "diff": round(float((c - o).mean()), 4), "diff_ci95": boot(c - o, rng), **mcnemar(c, o),
 120:           "passes": rep["b_eval"]["benefit"]["passes"]}
 121:     pr["success"] = bool(gate and c.mean() > o.mean() and pr["p"] < 0.05 and pr["passes"] <= MAX_PASSES)
 122:     pr["status"] = "not testable (iterative gain < 0.02)" if not gate else ("holds" if pr["success"] else "fails")
 123:     rep["primary"] = pr
 124:     h = H["hop_exp13"]
 125:     rep["secondary"] = {"benefit_vs_hop_router": {"diff": round(float((c - h).mean()), 4), "diff_ci95": boot(c - h, rng), **mcnemar(c, h)},
 126:                         "benefit_vs_iterative": {"diff": round(float((c - i).mean()), 4), "diff_ci95": boot(c - i, rng)}}
 127:     s = rep["secondary"]
 128:     s["beats_hop_router"] = bool(c.mean() > h.mean() and s["benefit_vs_hop_router"]["p"] < 0.05)
 129:     s["noninferior_to_iterative"] = bool(s["benefit_vs_iterative"]["diff_ci95"][0] > -0.01)
 130: 
 131:     # Descriptive: the same frozen router + tau on dev_fresh (re-used set from exp13; labelled re-use)
 132:     if (RES / "rank_dev_fresh.jsonl").exists():
 133:         rows, R = ranks("dev_fresh"); route = reg.predict(feats_benefit(rows, R["ctrl_oneshot"])) >= tau
 134:         orders, ps = cascade(rows, R, route)
 135:         o2, i2, c2 = (hits(rows, R["ctrl_oneshot"]["orders"]), hits(rows, R["iter_iter"]["orders"]), hits(rows, orders))
 136:         rep["dev_fresh_reuse"] = {"oneshot": round(float(o2.mean()), 4), "iterative": round(float(i2.mean()), 4),
 137:                                   "benefit_cascade": round(float(c2.mean()), 4), "passes": round(ps, 1), "routed": round(float(route.mean()), 3),
 138:                                   "cascade_vs_oneshot": mcnemar(c2, o2)}
 139:     (RES / "results.json").write_text(json.dumps(rep, indent=1))
 140:     print(json.dumps(rep, indent=1))
 141: 
 142: 
 143: if __name__ == "__main__":
 144:     main()

### experiments/exp14_benefit_router/data.py
   1: """MuSiQue-Ans loading, paragraph format and prompt (copied from exp08; data files read from ../exp08_musique/data)."""
   2: 
   3: from __future__ import annotations
   4: 
   5: import json
   6: from functools import lru_cache
   7: from pathlib import Path
   8: 
   9: HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
  10: FILES = {"dev": "musique_ans_v1.0_dev.jsonl", "pool": "musique_ans_v1.0_train.jsonl"}
  11: SPLITS = {"b_train": "pool", "b_tune": "pool", "b_eval": "pool", "router_train": "pool", "dev_fresh": "dev"}  # split -> source file
  12: 
  13: 
  14: @lru_cache(maxsize=None)
  15: def _file(src: str) -> dict:
  16:     with open(HERE.parent / "exp08_musique" / "data" / FILES[src], encoding="utf-8") as fh:
  17:         return {r["id"]: r for r in map(json.loads, fh)}
  18: 
  19: 
  20: def load(split: str) -> list[dict]:
  21:     ids = json.loads((HERE / "split_ids.json").read_text())[split]
  22:     rows = _file(SPLITS[split])
  23:     return [rows[i] for i in ids]
  24: 
  25: 
  26: def paragraphs_of(row: dict) -> tuple[list[str], list[str]]:
  27:     """'<title>. <text>' as in exp02."""
  28:     ps = sorted(row["paragraphs"], key=lambda p: p["idx"])
  29:     titles = [p["title"].strip() for p in ps]
  30:     return titles, [f"{t}. {p['paragraph_text'].strip()}" for t, p in zip(titles, ps)]
  31: 
  32: 
  33: def gold_mask(row: dict) -> list[int]:
  34:     return [int(p["is_supporting"]) for p in sorted(row["paragraphs"], key=lambda p: p["idx"])]
  35: 
  36: 
  37: def hops(row: dict) -> int:
  38:     return len(row["question_decomposition"])
  39: 
  40: 
  41: def build_retrieval_prompt(question: str, paragraphs: list[str]) -> str:
  42:     """exp02/BRACIS prompt; only change: 'the HotpotQA question' -> 'the question'."""
  43:     context_str = "\n\n".join([f"[{i + 1}] {p}" for i, p in enumerate(paragraphs)])
  44:     return f"""Answer the question using only the provided context.
  45: 
  46: Rules:
  47: - Output only the final answer.
  48: - If the answer is yes, no or noanswer, output exactly: yes or no or noanswer.
  49: - Otherwise output a short span or entity name only.
  50: - Do not include any explanation.
  51: - Do not repeat the question.
  52: - If multiple positions/titles are mentioned, output only the one that
  53:   directly answers the question.
  54: 
  55: Context:
  56: {context_str}
  57: 
  58: Question: {question}
  59: 
  60: Answer:"""
  61: 
  62: 
  63: _ENC = None
  64: 
  65: 
  66: def n_tokens(text: str) -> int:
  67:     global _ENC
  68:     if _ENC is None:
  69:         import tiktoken
  70: 
  71:         _ENC = tiktoken.get_encoding("o200k_base")
  72:     return len(_ENC.encode(text))
  73: 
  74: 
  75: def chain(row: dict) -> list[int]:
  76:     """Gold paragraph positions (in paragraphs_of order) in decomposition (hop) order."""
  77:     pos = {p["idx"]: i for i, p in enumerate(sorted(row["paragraphs"], key=lambda p: p["idx"]))}
  78:     return [pos[d["paragraph_support_idx"]] for d in row["question_decomposition"]]
  79: 
  80: 
  81: def query(question: str, selected: list[str]) -> str:
  82:     """Question + selected paragraphs, most recent first (truncation cuts the oldest)."""
  83:     return " [SEP] ".join([question, *reversed(selected)])
  84: 
  85: 
  86: def encode(tok, qs, ps, max_length=512):
  87:     """Truncate the query side only (PROTOCOL); if a paragraph alone exceeds max_length, fall back to longest_first."""
  88:     try:
  89:         return tok(qs, ps, truncation="only_first", max_length=max_length, padding=True, return_tensors="pt")
  90:     except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - tokenizers raises a bare Exception for this case
  91:         return tok(qs, ps, truncation="longest_first", max_length=max_length, padding=True, return_tensors="pt")
### experiments/exp13_cascade/README.md
   1: # exp13: 4-hop confirmation and a one-shot â†’ iterative cascade
   2: 
   3: Pre-registered in [PROTOCOL.md](PROTOCOL.md) (commit d071b74, revised after an adversarial Codex pre-run review).
   4: - **Models:** exp12's frozen GPU models. M1-iter and M1-ctrl were trained on identical pairs.
   5: - **Data:** fresh questions never used before (seed-13 splits).
   6: - **Compute:** Colab T4, code pinned to d071b74. The analysis is pre-written (`analyze.py`).
   7: - **Results:** [`results/results.json`](results/results.json), with per-question hits and router decisions in [`results/hits_compact.json`](results/hits_compact.json). Both were checked locally: the primary recomputed from the hits matches exactly.
   8: 
   9: ## Bottom line
  10: 
  11: 1. **Primary confirmed: on fresh 4-hop chains, conditioning-trained iteration beats matched one-shot ranking.** n = 566, recall@5 **0.500 vs 0.445, +0.055 [+0.007, +0.102]**, 117 vs 86 discordant questions, **p = 0.035**. Both strata are positive, so the success rule is met.
  12:    - **The strata differ a lot:**
  13:      - dev 4-hop (n = 166): **+0.139** (p = 0.006)
  14:      - train 4-hop (n = 400): **+0.020** (n.s.)
  15:    - **Seeds:** +0.055 / +0.044 / +0.016, all positive.
  16:    - **Size:** the effect is at the pre-registered minimum worthwhile effect (+0.05), not the +0.19 seen after the fact in exp12, as the stated prior expected.
  17: 2. **The cascade fails both secondaries.**
  18:    - **Router:** routes 29% of questions; 4-hop recall 0.76, precision 0.43.
  19:    - **Cascade:** recall@5 0.702 at 41.5 passes, against one-shot 0.693 at 20 and always-iterative 0.745 at 74.
  20:      - vs one-shot: **+0.009 [âˆ’0.010, +0.027]**, p = 0.39 âœ—
  21:      - vs iterative: **âˆ’0.043 [âˆ’0.068, âˆ’0.020]**, not non-inferior âœ—
  22:    - **Even the oracle cascade loses:** routing exactly the 4-hop questions gives 0.716 at 32 passes. Iteration also helps 3-hop chains on these fresh questions, so a 4-hop-only router leaves gain on the table.
  23: 3. **Exploratory (not pre-registered, uncorrected): on all 1,017 fresh dev questions, iteration helps chains of 3 or more hops.**
  24: 
  25:    | Hops | Iterative | One-shot | Discordant | p |
  26:    |---|---|---|---|---|
  27:    | all | **0.745** | 0.693 | 151 / 98 | 0.0009 |
  28:    | 2 | 0.822 | 0.813 | 51 / 46 | 0.68 |
  29:    | 3 | **0.724** | 0.646 | 56 / 31 | 0.010 |
  30:    | 4 | **0.542** | 0.404 | 44 / 21 | 0.006 |
  31: 
  32:    This is larger than exp12's overall +0.04 on its 400 test questions (n.s.). Taken together, the evidence points to a real but modest gain from iteration that comes entirely from â‰¥ 3-hop chains. A pooled analysis would need to be pre-registered.
  33: 4. **The router picks questions that look hard, not questions where iteration helps.** It routed 45% of 3-hop questions, yet the cascade's 3-hop recall (0.640) equals one-shot's (0.646). Score shape and question length predict hop count, but not iteration benefit.
  34: 
  35: ## 2Ã—2 on fresh 4-hop chains (recall@5)
  36: 
  37: | | f4_dev: one-shot | f4_dev: iterative | f4_train: one-shot | f4_train: iterative |
  38: |---|---|---|---|---|
  39: | M1-iter | 0.337 | **0.542** | 0.393 | **0.483** |
  40: | M1-ctrl | **0.404** | 0.169 | **0.463** | 0.118 |
  41: 
  42: - Each model is best in the mode it was trained for, as in exp12.
  43: - 17â€“19% of the iterative 4-hop questions reach the 512-token limit at the last step, so the oldest picked paragraph is truncated.
  44: 
  45: ## What this changes
  46: 
  47: - **H6-4hop:** confirmed (C). The effect is modest (+0.055) and heterogeneous across sources.
  48: - **Cascade (H11):** N. A 4-hop router built from one-shot score features does not make a useful cascade.
  49: - **For the thesis:**
  50:   - *Research:* the conditioning skill (a rank-2 change, exp12) gives a real gain on chains of 3 or more hops, about +0.05 to +0.14 recall@5, and nothing on 2-hop chains.
  51:   - *Practical:* the obvious cascade fails. The open problem is predicting *where iteration helps*, not predicting hop count. A router trained on that benefit directly, labelled by running both rankers on a training split, is the next candidate.
  52: 
  53: ## Deviations
  54: 
  55: - **The router's cascade behavior was partly seen in a smoke test** before registration: question-length features with random scores gave 4-hop recall 0.75 and precision 0.41. This is disclosed in PROTOCOL, and nothing was changed.
  56: - **Colab reset its runtime once** while waiting for the Drive authorization, before any ranking. The run started from scratch in a fresh output folder.
  57: 
  58: ## Files
  59: 
  60: - `PROTOCOL.md`, `split_ids.json`, `rank.py`, `analyze.py`, `make_notebook.py`, `exp13_colab.ipynb`
  61: - `results/results.json`, `results/hits_compact.json`

## Adversarial pre-run review

I found no obvious overlap among the four train-derived splits: each has the stated size and unique IDs, and their pairwise intersections are empty (`split_ids.json`, single-line JSON). That does not establish that the source rows or model artifacts match the registered versions.

### Threats to a positive result

- **The “matched” comparison changes both model and inference mode.** The primary compares M1-iter used iteratively with M1-ctrl used one-shot ([PROTOCOL.md:14–19](</C:/code/qualiusp2026/experiments/exp14_benefit_router/PROTOCOL.md:14>)). Exp13 says the models used identical pairs ([README.md:4](</C:/code/qualiusp2026/experiments/exp13_cascade/README.md:4>)), but this does not isolate the benefit of iterative inference from the benefit of the iterative training objective. `rank.py` already defines `iter_oneshot` and `ctrl_iter` ([rank.py:27–30](</C:/code/qualiusp2026/experiments/exp14_benefit_router/rank.py:27>)); the notebook runs only `ctrl_oneshot` and `iter_iter` ([make_notebook.py:57–59](</C:/code/qualiusp2026/experiments/exp14_benefit_router/make_notebook.py:57>)). **Before running:** score those two extra configs on `b_eval` and report the 2×2; otherwise attribute the effect to the *model-plus-inference package*, not iteration alone.
- **Model identity is not enforced in the notebook.** It prints two model hash prefixes but does not assert expected hashes ([make_notebook.py:48–50](</C:/code/qualiusp2026/experiments/exp14_benefit_router/make_notebook.py:48>)); `rank.py` records whichever `model.safetensors` is present ([rank.py:69–81](</C:/code/qualiusp2026/experiments/exp14_benefit_router/rank.py:69>)). **Before running:** assert full expected hashes for both model files, and record tokenizer, Transformers, PyTorch, CUDA, and GPU versions.
- **Stale ranking files can be silently reused.** The notebook reuses exp13 router/dev rankings if destination files already exist ([make_notebook.py:54–56](</C:/code/qualiusp2026/experiments/exp14_benefit_router/make_notebook.py:54>)); `rank.py` skips a config with a matching model hash without checking its IDs ([rank.py:66–73](</C:/code/qualiusp2026/experiments/exp14_benefit_router/rank.py:66>)). The analysis checks IDs for the four analyzed splits, but the reused rankings are only indirectly protected ([analyze.py:27–36](</C:/code/qualiusp2026/experiments/exp14_benefit_router/analyze.py:27>)). **Before running:** require an empty output directory, or validate every reused file’s split ID list, model hash, tokenizer hash, and row count before accepting it.
- **Truncation is not audited.** Iterative queries grow with selected paragraphs; `encode` truncates the query to 512 tokens and falls back to `longest_first` if a paragraph alone is too long ([data.py:86–91](</C:/code/qualiusp2026/experiments/exp14_benefit_router/data.py:86>)). Exp13 reports the oldest selected paragraph is truncated at the last step for 17–19% of 4-hop questions ([README.md:42–43](</C:/code/qualiusp2026/experiments/exp13_cascade/README.md:42>)). `rank.py` logs an untruncated query length, but the analysis never uses it ([rank.py:44–54](</C:/code/qualiusp2026/experiments/exp14_benefit_router/rank.py:44>)). **Before running:** log effective encoded lengths/truncation per step and report primary results on truncated vs untruncated cases.
- **“Passes” may not match compute.** The ≤50 cap counts candidate scores, not tokens processed or wall time ([analyze.py:69–73](</C:/code/qualiusp2026/experiments/exp14_benefit_router/analyze.py:69>)); query lengths differ by step. **Before running:** report total input tokens and GPU seconds alongside passes.
- **Score features may partly encode length/truncation.** The router uses the one-shot top-10 scores and entropy ([analyze.py:54–62](</C:/code/qualiusp2026/experiments/exp14_benefit_router/analyze.py:54>)); that is allowed, but may proxy question or candidate-set artifacts rather than benefit. Cheap check: compare against a text-only router and report performance by hop count and truncation status.

### Decision-rule problems

- **Power is weak by the protocol’s own estimate:** roughly 50% for the assumed +0.015 effect ([PROTOCOL.md:46–49](</C:/code/qualiusp2026/experiments/exp14_benefit_router/PROTOCOL.md:46>)). A nonsignificant result would not establish no useful routing signal. The gate also makes “not testable” a possible outcome based on the same `b_eval` used for the primary ([analyze.py:117–123](</C:/code/qualiusp2026/experiments/exp14_benefit_router/analyze.py:117>)); report gate, effect, and CI regardless, and do not interpret a failed gate as evidence that benefit is unlearnable.
- **Threshold selection is noisy and coarse.** It selects the best cascade recall among up to 101 tune-set quantiles ([analyze.py:86–95](</C:/code/qualiusp2026/experiments/exp14_benefit_router/analyze.py:86>)). The independent eval prevents direct threshold leakage into the primary, but tune recall is optimistically selected and threshold stability is unknown. Cheap check: bootstrap `b_tune` to show the distribution of τ and routed fraction; keep eval untouched.
- **The CI is not the decision rule.** Primary success uses McNemar p<.05 and direction, but the stated worthwhile effect is +0.01 ([PROTOCOL.md:46–49](</C:/code/qualiusp2026/experiments/exp14_benefit_router/PROTOCOL.md:46>), [analyze.py:119–123](</C:/code/qualiusp2026/experiments/exp14_benefit_router/analyze.py:119>)). A tiny positive can pass. Add a requirement that the effect estimate or lower CI bound meets the predeclared worthwhile-effect criterion, and report the paired CI.
- **Secondary claims have no multiplicity adjustment.** “Beats hop router” and non-inferiority are both tested, plus multiple descriptive `k` and hop strata ([PROTOCOL.md:50–59](</C:/code/qualiusp2026/experiments/exp14_benefit_router/PROTOCOL.md:50>), [analyze.py:124–129](</C:/code/qualiusp2026/experiments/exp14_benefit_router/analyze.py:124>)). Keep them explicitly exploratory or predeclare a correction; do not treat either as confirmatory.
- **Non-inferiority uses a two-sided percentile bootstrap CI** and a tight −0.01 margin ([analyze.py:49–51](</C:/code/qualiusp2026/experiments/exp14_benefit_router/analyze.py:49>), [analyze.py:125–129](</C:/code/qualiusp2026/experiments/exp14_benefit_router/analyze.py:125>)). Predeclare a one-sided 95% paired CI method and justify the margin. The protocol does not define a recovery ratio or `r*`; if either will be reported, define its formula, CI, and selection rule before seeing eval.
- **Metric and input integrity deserve explicit checks.** `ranks()` checks IDs and permutations but not lengths of `orders`, `passes`, or `top10`; downstream `zip` can silently truncate ([analyze.py:27–41](</C:/code/qualiusp2026/experiments/exp14_benefit_router/analyze.py:27>)). Add exact length assertions and assert both ranking files use identical per-question paragraph counts. Also state clearly that “recall@5” means *all* supporting paragraphs appear in the top five ([analyze.py:40–41](</C:/code/qualiusp2026/experiments/exp14_benefit_router/analyze.py:40>)).
tokens used
38,211
